< 620765205||54354734||The first four pages of this book can be found on enemies scattered across Angmar, Eregion, and Moria. The last four pages of this book can be found on enemies scattered across Forochel, Moria, and the Misty Mountains.\n\nThis book was written as a primer in the Guardian's art some time in the middle of the Third Age, during the difficult period after the fall of Khazad-dûm. At that time, Durin's Folk were scattered and greatly reduced in number, and the Guardian's role took on a much greater importance as the threat of goblins and Orcs to dwarf-settlements beneath the roots of the world became more and more common. The dwarves often settled for building powerful strongholds designed to make the approach to their cities, impervious to assault, manning them with skilled and powerful guardians to ensure that none would pass unchallenged.\n\nWhile the book itself is common enough, the owner of this copy seems to have scribbled copious notes within the margins of the pages -- notes containing an experience and insight to the art that even at a glance exceeds that of the original author. Unfortunately, the copy is worn and tattered, and a number of pages are missing. Perhaps someone well-versed in the art could provide you with some insight as to the identity of the mysterious scribbler and where his remaining notes could be found?||NULL||NULL||1
< 620765205||218649169||'Well, it's right kind of you to think so highly of me as to look me up to know more about that book you have there, but I must say I still feel rather overwhelmed by all this, and it's not much in my nature to be galavanting about with a sword and all, if you know what I mean. Still, old Gandalf insisted that I take the time we have here in Rivendell to read up on such things and learn my duties and all before Master Frodo begins our...Well, before we have to go. I guess I really shouldn't say anything more about that -- Samwise, you ninnyhammer, Gandalf will turn you into a toad for sure if you don't shape up....\n\n'Anyways, Master Elrond suggested I read up on that book you have there -- he has a fine copy in his library, you know -- and it was an interesting read, sure enough, but that copy you've got there seems more interesting by half, as the fellow who took all those notes seems to have a fine handle on how it really goes when things heat up. Pity about the missing pages, it looks like the best parts are gone. If you manage to pull them together, I'd love to have a look at them, too...not that I'm like to have time for more reading what with us all getting ready to head off to....\n\n'Well, never you mind that, you've got your own business to see to like as not, and don't need me bothering you with ours, I suppose.'||NULL||NULL||1

> 620765205||54354734||The first four pages of this book can be found on enemies scattered across Angmar, Eregion, and Moria. The last four pages of this book can be found on enemies scattered across Forochel, Moria, and the Misty Mountains.\n\nThis book was written as a primer in the Guardian's art some time in the middle of the Third Age, during the difficult period after the fall of Khazad-dûm. At that time, Durin's Folk were scattered and greatly reduced in number, and the Guardian's role took on a much greater importance as the threat of goblins and Orcs to dwarf-settlements beneath the roots of the world became more and more common. The dwarves often settled for building powerful strongholds designed to make the approach to their cities impervious to assault, manning them with skilled and powerful guardians to ensure that none would pass unchallenged.\n\nWhile the book itself is common enough, the owner of this copy seems to have scribbled copious notes within the margins of the pages -- notes containing an experience and insight to the art that even at a glance exceeds that of the original author. Unfortunately, the copy is worn and tattered, and a number of pages are missing. Perhaps someone well-versed in the art could provide you with some insight as to the identity of the mysterious scribbler and where his remaining notes could be found?||NULL||NULL||1
> 620765205||218649169||'Well, it's right kind of you to think so highly of me as to look me up to know more about that book you have there, but I must say I still feel rather overwhelmed by all this, and it's not much in my nature to be gallivanting about with a sword and all, if you know what I mean. Still, old Gandalf insisted that I take the time we have here in Rivendell to read up on such things and learn my duties and all before Master Frodo begins our... well, before we have to go. I guess I really shouldn't say anything more about that -- Samwise, you ninnyhammer, Gandalf will turn you into a toad for sure if you don't shape up....\n\n'Anyways, Master Elrond suggested I read up on that book you have there -- he has a fine copy in his library, you know -- and it was an interesting read, sure enough, but that copy you've got there seems more interesting by half, as the fellow who took all those notes seems to have a fine handle on how it really goes when things heat up. Pity about the missing pages, it looks like the best parts are gone. > 621120974||228284034||'Greetings to you, adventurer. Yes, I see you for what you are: a voyager across many lands and a doer of great deeds! I am a travelling story-teller. The name Lakhêpi the Far-faring is known far and wide across Shagâna and Jarmát; she sings songs and tells tales to put food in her belly and a roof over her head. She is I, and I am her!\n\n'But I have run into some trouble of late, for I was turned away from Emax Dûl due to some... recent unpleasantness. > 621121254||162883719||'Yes, this story is the most complex of all. To the people of Pahar Hatokáli, Shâra Mizâdi is a shining figure who fights before a willing army to free them from the shackles of Ordâkh: she is a symbol of freedom.\n\n'But she is a woman too, and one who has suffered grief and sorrows enough to cause many others to give up her mission. Yet she fights on. I cannot say if her story is a triumph or a tragedy. I wonder what she would say herself?'\n\nLakhêpi the Far-faring gazes at you expectantly, and you spread your arms wide: you have exhausted the stories you collected. She smiles.\n\n'You have done well, friend helper. These stories will keep me going for many days to come, and I thank you for them, and for your friendship. In honour of that friendship, I will keep the worrying tales of Mashdagâl's ancient mind-tamperers to myself. It is the least I can do.'||NULL||NULL||1

> 621121794||137079169||The forces of Renewed Hamât have established the war camp of Ingarûma on a naturally rocky rise midway through Pahar Hatokáli. Many throughout the years have sought to establish camps here for the commanding view it provides of the surrounding fells. Will Shâra Mizâdi be the last?\n\nSoldiers, provisioners, forge-masters, strategists, and scouts have made this their outpost in lands far from their homes. Most are eager to put an end to Bârshud and the forces he commands so they may leave this place, but until that day comes they will endure.||NULL||NULL||1

> 621121794||218639270||The village of Hanamíku serves as a way-station nestled among the rocks of Nêr Abatu, the Gate of Stones that stands between Idagâl and Pahar Hatokáli. It has been a place of peace during the years since the Ordâkhai came north and took up residence at Emax Dûl, but that has changed almost overnight.\n\nSoldiers of Ordâkh fleeing the city passed through Hanamíku on their way south, pursued by the army of Renewed Hamât. Bârshud's forces burned farms and wrought devastation as they fled, seeking to deprive Shâra Mizâdi of help and supplies. Now the villagers fear any who bear weapons, and many mistrust outsiders.||NULL||NULL||1
